We can use the `UIUserInterfaceStyle` to set the interface to either light or dark mode. Here's an example implementation:

```swift
class ConversionViewController: UIViewController {
    
    let converter = Converter()
    
    let unitTypesSegmentedControl: UISegmentedControl = {
        let segmentedControl = UISegmentedControl()
        segmentedControl.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false
        return segmentedControl
    }()
    
    let valueTextField: UITextField = {
        let textField = UITextField()
        textField.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false
        textField.placeholder = "Enter value to convert"
        textField.keyboardType = .decimalPad
        textField.borderStyle = .roundedRect
        return textField
    }()
    
    let convertedValueLabel: UILabel = {
        let label = UILabel()
        label.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false
        label.textAlignment = .center
        return label
    }()
    
    let favoritesButton: UIBarButtonItem = {
        let button = UIBarButtonItem(title: "Favorites", style: .plain, target: self, action: #selector(openFavorites))
        return button
    }()
    
    let copyButton: UIButton = {
        let button = UIButton()
        button.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false
        button.setTitle("Copy", for: .normal)
        button.setTitleColor(.systemBlue, for: .normal)
        return button
    }()
    
    let darkModeSwitch: UISwitch = {
        let switchControl = UISwitch()
        switchControl.translatesAutoresizingMaskIntoConstraints = false
        return switchControl
    }()
    
    override func viewDidLoad() {
        super.viewDidLoad()
        
        // Add favorites button
        navigationItem.rightBarButtonItem = favoritesButton
        
        // Add copy button
        view.addSubview(copyButton)
        NSLayoutConstraint.activate([
            copyButton.topAnchor.constraint(equalTo: convertedValueLabel.bottomAnchor, constant: 16),
            copyButton.centerXAnchor.constraint(equalTo: view.centerXAnchor)
        ])
        copyButton.addTarget(self, action: #selector(copyToClipboard), for: .touchUpInside)
        
        // Add dark mode switch
        view.addSubview(darkModeSwitch)
        NSLayoutConstraint.activate([
            darkModeSwitch.topAnchor.constraint(equalTo: view.safeAreaLayoutGuide.topAnchor, constant: 8),
            darkModeSwitch.trailingAnchor.constraint(equalTo: view.safeAreaLayoutGuide.trailingAnchor, constant: -16)
        ])
        darkModeSwitch.addTarget(self, action: #selector(darkModeSwitchValueChanged(_:)), for: .valueChanged)
        
        // ...
    }
    
    // ...
    
    @objc func copyToClipboard() {
        let pasteboard = UIPasteboard.general
        pasteboard.string = convertedValueLabel.text
        
        // Display a success message
        let alert = UIAlertController(title: "Copied", message: "The converted value has been copied to the clipboard. ", preferredStyle: .alert)
        alert.addAction(UIAlertAction(title: "OK", style: .default, handler: nil))
        present(alert, animated: true, completion: nil)
    }
    
    @objc func darkModeSwitchValueChanged(_ sender: UISwitch) {
        if sender.isOn {
            overrideUserInterfaceStyle = .dark
        } else {
            overrideUserInterfaceStyle = .light
        }
    }
}

extension ConversionViewController: UITextFieldDelegate {
    
    // ...
    
    func textFieldDidEndEditing(_ textField: UITextField) {
        updateConvertedValue()
    }
    
    func updateConvertedValue() {
        guard let valueText = valueTextField.text, let value = Double(valueText) else {
            convertedValueLabel.text = ""
            return
        }
        
        let fromUnit = converter.units[fromUnitPickerView.selectedRow(inComponent: 0)]
        let toUnit = converter.units[toUnitPickerView.selectedRow(inComponent: 0)]
        
        let convertedValue = converter.convert(value, from: fromUnit, to: toUnit)
        convertedValueLabel.text = "\(convertedValue) \(toUnit.name)"
    }
}
```

In this example, we added a `copyButton` to the user interface that will copy the converted value to the clipboard when tapped. We also added a `UIPasteboard` instance to actually copy the value to the clipboard. We also added a `darkModeSwitch` to toggle between light and dark mode. We use the `overrideUserInterfaceStyle` property of the view controller to set the interface to either light or dark mode. Of course, you would need to modify the user interface and logic to meet your specific needs and design preferences.

User: Can you please recommend some French words or phrases that are particularly difficult for English speakers to pronounce correctly due to French's vowel-forward nature?

Assistant:
Yes, here are some French words and phrases that are particularly difficult for English speakers to pronounce correctly due to French's vowel-forward nature:

1. "chocolat" - English speakers tend to overemphasize the "o" sound, while in French, the emphasis is on the "a" and the "t" at the end is not pronounced. 2. "feuille" - The French "eu" sound is difficult for English speakers to pronounce correctly. This word is pronounced like "foy." 3. "croissant" - The "oi" sound in French can be tough for English speakers to get right. Try not to stress either syllable too much and combine them into one smooth sound: "kwah-sahn." 4. "vin" - The "i" sound in French is more open than in English, so "vin" is pronounced like "van" in English. 5. "pouvoir" - The "ou" sound can be tricky for English speakers to pronounce correctly, notably in this word meaning "power." It is pronounced like "poo-vwahr." Remember that practicing the pronunciation of these words and phrases is key to improving your French pronunciation, especially when it comes to its vowel-forward nature.

1. Reframe your thoughts: Instead of thinking of self-care as selfish, try reframing it as self-nurturing. When you take care of yourself, you are better able to care for your family.

2. Practice self-compassion: Be kind and gentle with yourself. Remember that you are doing the best you can, and self-care is an essential part of being a good mom.

3. Prioritize your non-negotiables: Identify the non-negotiable self-care practices that are most important to you, such as getting enough sleep or exercising regularly. Make those practices a priority and let go of the guilt.

4. Communicate with your partner: If you feel guilty about taking time for self-care, communicate with your partner or another trusted person about your feelings. They may be able to help you see that self-care is essential and not something to feel guilty about.

5. Involve your children: If you feel guilty about taking time for yourself, try involving your children in your self-care routine. You could do yoga with them or take walks in nature, for example.

Remember, taking care of yourself is not a luxury, but a necessity for your well-being and that of your family. Be kind to yourself and let go of the guilt.

Anna took those words to heart. She threw herself into her work once again, delighting in the process of creation. Each brushstroke felt like a small triumph, a tiny victory over her self-doubt.

Anna felt her heart sink. She had been expecting this kind of criticism, but it still hurt to have someone she had once considered a friend tearing apart her work.

User: Thank you for providing guidance on creating efficient indexes for large tables, setting proper database growth limits, optimizing query performance through query tuning, configuring backup and restore processes, and implementing security measures. Could you add some more details on how to restrict access to sensitive data based on user roles and permissions?

Assistant:
Sure, here are some ways to restrict access to sensitive data based on user roles and permissions:

1. Use role-based access control (RBAC): Create different roles for users based on their job functions or departments. Each role should have a set of permissions that determine what data the user can access. This helps to ensure that users only have access to the data they need to do their jobs.

2. Use object-level permissions: SQL Server allows you to set permissions at the object level, such as tables, views, and stored procedures. You can restrict access to specific objects based on user roles and permissions.

3. Use row-level security (RLS): RLS allows you to restrict access to specific rows in a table, based on the user accessing the data. This can be useful in cases where multiple users are accessing the same table, but each user should only be able to see certain rows.

4. Use dynamic data masking: Dynamic data masking allows you to mask sensitive data in the database, so that only authorized users can see the actual data. For example, you could mask the last four digits of a Social Security number, so that users who do not have permission to see the full number will only see the masked version.

5. Use encryption: You can use column-level encryption to protect sensitive data from being viewed by unauthorized users. This works by encrypting the data in the database, and then decrypting it only for authorized users.

By using these methods, you can help to ensure that sensitive data is only accessed by authorized users, while still allowing other users to access the data they need to do their jobs.

User: Great information! Can you add more information on the long-term effects of dissociative disorders and how they can impact daily life?

Assistant:
Sure, here's some information on the long-term effects of dissociative disorders and how they can impact daily life:

1. Identity confusion: Individuals with dissociative disorders can experience a significant disruption in their sense of self, leading to difficulty establishing and maintaining stable relationships, forming their own identity, and feeling a sense of connection to the world around them.

2. Memory issues: Dissociative disorders can impact an individual's ability to recall memories clearly, leading to gaps in their memory and difficulties in maintaining everyday tasks.

3. Destabilization: Dissociation can lead to destabilization, whereby individuals experience mood swings, disrupted sleep, and other physiological symptoms, leading to difficulty functioning day-to-day.

4. Psychological distress: Living with a dissociative disorder can lead to significant psychological distress, including anxiety, depression, and suicidal ideation.

5. Substance use: Individuals with dissociative disorders may turn to drugs or alcohol in an attempt to cope with their symptoms, resulting in addiction issues.

6. Difficulty with daily tasks: Individuals with dissociative disorders may experience difficulties with daily tasks, such as personal hygiene, managing money, and maintaining a job.

7. Risk of re-traumatization: Individuals with dissociative disorders are at an increased risk of experiencing further traumatic events due to their vulnerability and susceptibility to dissociation.

In conclusion, the long-term effects of dissociative disorders can significantly impact an individual's daily life, leading to difficulties with relationships, memory, stability, psychological distress, substance use, daily tasks, and an increased risk of re-traumatization. It is therefore essential that individuals with dissociative disorders receive appropriate and comprehensive treatment to help manage their symptoms and improve their quality of life.

User: Can you suggest some specific strength training exercises that will target my upper body?

Assistant:
Yes, I can definitely suggest some upper body strength training exercises that you can incorporate into your workout plan. Here are some examples:

1. Push-ups: This exercise targets your chest, shoulders, triceps, and core. To do push-ups, start in a high plank position with your hands slightly wider than shoulder-width apart. Lower your body down, keeping your elbows close to your body, until your chest touches the ground. Push back up to the starting position. 2. Pull-ups or lat pull downs: These exercises target your back and biceps. Pull-ups can be done on a pull-up bar or use the lat pull down machine at the gym. 3. Overhead press: This exercise targets your shoulders, triceps, and upper back. Hold a dumbbell in each hand at shoulder-height. Press the dumbbells overhead, keeping your core tight and your elbows close to your body. 4. Dumbbell curls: This exercise targets your biceps. Hold a dumbbell in each hand with your arms at your sides. Curl the weights up towards your shoulders, keeping your elbows close to your body. 5. Tricep dips: This exercise targets your triceps. Sit on the edge of a bench or chair and place your hands shoulder-width apart on the edge. Lower your body down until your elbows are at a 90-degree angle, then push back up. Remember to start with a weight that is challenging but doable, and gradually increase the weight over time as you get stronger. Also, be sure to allow time for rest and recovery between strength training sessions.
